## When Healing Turns Into Checking

You can get hooked on healing too. The wound can be completely real while you're checking it for the hundredth time to see whether you've finally grieved it correctly.

<!-- Turn 74 (his message of 2026-10-19 00:08 UTC (experiments/t74-answers/JOEL-20261019-0008.md)): 62, C ("it's human now, i fixed it, pasted"): his Before You Try to Go Deep as he pasted it at 00:08 UTC; this paragraph is his version (experiments/t74-guide/joel-before-you-try-20261019-0008.txt). It has 55, 2, A in fewer words. -->

Going back can still matter, even when nothing new turns up. But returning to the same wound can also become a search for certainty in an inherently uncertain existence. Spending all day asking, "Did I process it properly? Did I grieve it enough?" is clearly not what we're after here.

<!-- Turn 72 (his answers of 2026-10-18 19:06 UTC (experiments/t72-answers/JOEL-20261018-1906.md)): 54, 4 ("keep first instance, remove 2nd one in also look outward"; his 19:26 UTC "yes you read me correctly"): the guide's "A checking loop can move into behavior." with its examples and "The test may calm uncertainty briefly and then make the next fluctuation matter even more." ("exposure" and the exposure paragraph stay out, 54, 5, A). experiments/t72-guide/drafts/chk-beh-a.txt. Alone Human Written (0.091), https://www.pangram.com/history/863f2148-9ae1-4f18-978a-c5b77b1f4285 (round t72a); Before You Try to Go Deep with it (in its old order) Human Written (0.086, high, 3944 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/cd6d0971-e437-4404-8482-5a90c7c9b856 (round t72d). -->

<!-- Turn 74 (his message of 2026-10-19 00:08 UTC (experiments/t74-answers/JOEL-20261019-0008.md)): 62, C ("it's human now, i fixed it, pasted"): his Before You Try to Go Deep as he pasted it at 00:08 UTC; this paragraph is his version (experiments/t74-guide/joel-before-you-try-20261019-0008.txt). His own fix of the opening that kept a 37-word AI window (question 62), word for word, his comma after "Even" included. -->

And the checking doesn't always stay in your head. A body scan can turn into one, or a confession. Even, going on a date, or checking your dating profile matches, can be a version of this if what you're really after is proof that you're good and lovable. It might calm things down for a while, and then the next little wobble matters even more.

If you're feeling guilty because you didn't put your seat belt on, just put it on. If you still want to investigate it later, fine. Perhaps now and then you feel bad about that time you refused to put it on, broke your collar bone in a crash, and then got blamed for not wearing it. That's natural, but doesn't mean there's something new to look at. Then the background checker goes, “But are you really listening?” There it is. Seat belt's on. 😇 Where were we?

### How Would You Know It's Helping?

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E11 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e11 a") -->

Don't be afraid to ask your therapist/yourself "What is this practice supposed to help, and how would I know if it's helping?" This sovereign, gently curious stance should apply to everything in life. Feeling calmer or having deeper insights or explanations for the trauma response are some possible signs of initial progress, but if that's the endgame, it's just a cope. Real healing is ultimately measured by reduced need for the therapy. It's like real parenting: you might start out reading parenting books and thinking hard about what to say or do, but after a while you get into the groove and a lot of it becomes natural. Inner parenting can be like that too. You don't need to keep checking this guide or planning every conversation with your little one; the nurturing, protecting and guiding become more automatic. You might still turn to therapy when you need it, and life will keep offering chances to grow wiser and more compassionate. And if the therapy is actually working, it should improve your daily life, and especially the health of the inner child-inner adult relationship. Going at it harder isn't as useful as going at it with more wisdom and compassion. Take the parts that work, and innovate on the rest.

If these exercises start feeling like a ritual you have to complete before you're allowed to live, stop there.

## Catch the Hook Before the Story Takes Over

<!-- 2026-09-27: "the hook" is now introduced before it's used (Joel 04:19); Pema's sentence moved to the front with her own gloss. Joel's items 7 and 8 are in P3. Pangram 4.0: P1 alone 100% Human (96 words); the section 100% Human (255 words). Record: experiments/OWNER-PUNCHLIST-20260927.md. -->

Pema Chödrön calls the tightening and urge you get before the story has finished forming "shenpa," and she says "[hooked](https://www.lionsroar.com/how-we-get-hooked-shenpa-and-how-we-get-unhooked/)" is a more descriptive translation than the usual "attachment." You might notice the hook before you have any clue what part of you is doing the hooking. Or you might notice it embarrassingly late. The jaw and stomach are already tight, and/or half the text is written, and then suddenly it dawns on you: you're supposed to be observing yourself. 😀 Maybe forget the parts detective work for a minute.

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 97.2 A (his "97. i agree cut all"): cut "If nobody is in danger, take a breath" (with "Take a breath" for it). The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

When you catch it, look around and just take a moment to enjoy some free honesty: "Oh! I'm hooked again! That's awesome I could notice it!" Take a breath or three before you finish whatever you were doing. Sometimes human beings need to be being, not always doing. 🙃 Maybe then you'll find there's enough adult around to be nice to your little boo for a second. Or enough Protector to stop something you'd regret later.

(Joel: "it makes no sense AND it sounds ai with 'may have X and still Y'"); one punctuation fix, a double space in "Just  because". ### What to Make of a Passing Feeling

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E12 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e12 a") -->

Here's where I want to clearly depart from the new-age wizdumb that all your problems are self-created: Somebody may really have crossed a boundary. That's not just because they are your karmic partner mirroring what God wants to show you in yourself, even if that also applies sometimes. Your reaction is what you can control the most, though. If what they did hit something old in you, notice how that feels in your body. And notice how your feelings can also amplify or distort your perceptions of others. Just because your stomach tightened up doesn't mean their boundary crossing indicates “They're evil.” You can defer that judgement for when you're in your happy place.

Feelings about yourself work the same way. "I feel attracted" or "I feel ashamed" is real as a feeling, but it doesn't settle who you are, or who you love. Making too much out of something momentary is one side of the human psyche's pendulum. Give yourself time to see what patterns form more durably, and what feels authentically yours vs something planted or manipulated into being there from society, influencers, family, friends, religion, etc., etc. You may find an exceptional freedom in exactly that in-between place where you don't have to decide what your pronouns are, or whether you believe in this or that. If you stay in flux forever, though, it can seem like there's nothing solid about you. In that case, sometimes it helps to meditate or talk with a good unbiased therapist who can help you see the pattern.

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 97.3 A (his "97. i agree cut all"): cut "Same if something might be dangerous right now, or might be a medical warning sign: step away or get checked first.". The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

The opposite extreme of our pendulum, throwing everything transient away, as some people do with "thought-noting" practice, also isn't healthy, except as a meditation exercise. Transient feelings and thoughts can be meaningful signals. For example, if you don't want to be touched, say no now and work out what it means later.

Can you believe it?"). Alone Human Written (0.013), https://www.pangram.com/history/4a3b8e87-47e0-436b-945a-5895f37119e1; the h2 Human Written (0.0052, high), https://www.pangram.com/history/8e8d9cf1-d8f1-4ed3-93a8-e84ae1990fa1. -->

With a little warmth to work with, rest one hand at the center of your chest (the heart chakra) and feel for love, and the other on the solar plexus, just below, and feel for your little one. The solar plexus is the most vulnerable part of the torso. One punch there hurts a lot, as I sadly learned while getting beaten up every day at kindergarten recess by a gang organized by my best friend. Can you believe it? So it makes sense to look for your little one there.

<!-- Turn 65: love going down to your little one and around again, with its grounding's fixes (GROUNDING-sp3.md): the love coming back is theirs to give and not owed, as the vow says, and if none comes you keep sending yours; no rescue frame; with no warmth, rest the hands and notice. Alone Human Written (0.41, low), https://www.pangram.com/history/ee0c8587-57ce-473c-b26a-f1e7d6236055; the h2 Human (0.041, medium), https://www.pangram.com/history/2f59d723-4500-47f4-b003-808c3a553b35. Cold read OK. The h1 as installed (Borrow Hindsight, Remember When, the loop) read Human Written (0.099, high), https://www.pangram.com/history/aa97d3db-6dc0-4fda-bfdb-4f93b26d9a61. Record: experiments/t65-guide/REVIEWS.md. -->

<!-- Turn 68 (E188; his answer to question 40, A): his "Non-judgement is a place to start. It can blossom into love over time." from his October guide, at the end. Alone Human Written (0.11), https://www.pangram.com/history/36aa2411-2b25-435f-95d8-f0dd8145820a. -->

Picture the love traveling from your heart down to your little one. If some comes back up, let it, and send it on down again, so it goes around like a loop. That part's a gift from them, and they don't owe it to you, so if nothing comes back, just keep sending yours. No warmth today? Then rest your hands there and notice what you find, no faking required. Non-judgement is a place to start. It can blossom into love over time.

## Journal With Each Hand

<!-- Turn 71 (the overnight run): the guide's two hands (the non-dominant, clumsy one for the vulnerable feelings, the usual one answering as the grown-up), getting past the silence people hit with talking or picturing, and the protective part getting that hand first. It opens on a pointer of mine to the practice's best-known teacher, Lucia Capacchione, and her book The Power of Your Other Hand (her site, opened 2026-10-18, lists it; not in the guide, so question 50 asks whether he wants it), and the rest grows out of the title. Twenty-odd P1s without it read AI (turns 65 and 71). experiments/t71-guide/drafts/jw-s.txt. Alone Human Written (0.0062, medium), https://www.pangram.com/history/895d5d72-8908-4813-abfd-e8758ed06d98 (round t71kk); the h2 below. -->

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 98.5 A (his "98. agree cut all"): cut "(nothing made up)". The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

Lucia Capacchione, an art therapist, wrote a whole book on this one, [The Power of Your Other Hand](https://www.luciac.com/). As titles go, it's pretty literal. Your other hand, the clumsy one, writes like a first grader anyway, so it gets the scared or hurt feelings. The usual hand answers, as the grown-up. Some people who get nothing but silence from their little one are surprised by what the other hand comes up with. A protective part might grab the pen first, and then it gets that hand until it's had its say.

Its last four sentences are turn 65's writer's (experiments/t65-guide/writers/sp4-w1-p2.txt), with its list cut to two ("a dated note of what happened, or of what helped"); the opening question and the line after it are mine. experiments/t71-guide/drafts/jw2-g.txt. Alone Human Written (0.085), https://www.pangram.com/history/747a5d97-6357-49d7-8b76-2fc936018255; the h2 Human Written (0.018, high, 179 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/eb705c60-c368-4de9-8283-e9b7fc78ec5f (round t71nn). -->

And what if all that writing leaves you more confused than when you started? Then just keep track for a while, and save the patterns for later, if they turn out to matter at all. A memory aid doesn't have to explain your life. It could be a dated note of what happened, or of what helped. A reminder or a next step works too. Or you can leave yourself a voice note.

On a steadier day, when thinking about other people won't pull you out of your own shoes, name the feeling plainly (lonely, say) and think of how many people are feeling that exact thing right now, and wanting it to stop, just like you. With that many people feeling the same thing, the theory that you're the defective one gets kind of hard to keep up. Some of them make it worse trying to get away from it. Just like you, sometimes. 😅 Wish all of you, you first, enough support to stay with it, so the pain stops with you.

If you've tried Pema Chödrön's [tonglen](https://www.lionsroar.com/how-to-practice-tonglen-meditation/), this is a gentler cousin, and normal breathing is fine. Whoever hurt you might come to mind, too. I'd leave out forgiving them and picturing their childhood.

## “And So That’s the Way It Happened!”

<!-- Turn 71 (E189, E190): his published line about Xandria first (question 43, 5: "my words", Joel 2026-10-17 22:59 UTC, experiments/t70-answers/JOEL-20261017-2259.md), then my humanized version of the rest of the guide's paragraph (experiments/t69-guide/drafts/sp5-p1-x1.txt): say it the way a grandpa would finish a story on the porch, "Once upon a time…" if the start is where you get stuck, out of the courtroom and into your life story, a memory you can handle for the first few tries. Alone Human Written (0.0063), https://www.pangram.com/history/29e78b5c-24af-4528-b385-6beadd266412 (turn 69); P1 and P2 together Human Written (0.056, medium, 146 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/ba400925-6f0a-41c8-b029-8aea387ee5ca (round t71a); the h2 Human Written (0.24, high, 226 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/c9f9e639-32af-46ea-a863-1bb460af2d8e (round t71c). Record: experiments/t71-guide/REVIEWS.md. -->

My ex, Xandria, taught me to append “And so that’s the way it happened!” to a difficult memory. Say it the way a grandpa would finish a story on the porch. ("Once upon a time…" helps if the start is where you get stuck.) Somehow it moves the whole thing out of the courtroom, where the case was still being argued, and into your life story. Pick a memory you can handle for the first few tries.

<!-- Turn 70 (E190, his moves: a spoken answer to the question, both sides said): saying it doesn't make the memory fiction, it's still what happened as far as you remember it, though details may be fuzzy; nobody's off the hook, you included, and any repair that's owed is still owed (experiments/t70-guide/drafts/sp5-p2-y1.txt). Turn 71, the cold read's two fixes (experiments/t71-guide/COLD-READ-t71-altered.md): "the one who did the harm" for "the one who did it" (nothing had named the harm), and "If the phrase does the trick, leave it at that, no need to narrate the whole thing." for "If the line helps, keep it to the one line." (two lines had been offered); experiments/t71-guide/drafts/y1-b.txt. Does saying it make the memory fiction? Well, no, it's still what happened, as far as you remember it, even if some details turn out fuzzy. If the phrase does the trick, leave it at that, no need to narrate the whole thing.

<!-- Turn 71: the guide's stop signs and its last step (experiments/t71-guide/drafts/x3-c.txt): keep an ear on who's narrating; if the voice starts sneering at the younger you, stop (the courtroom creeping back in, P1's image again); stop too if you feel more ashamed, or floaty and unreal, since more distance won't help with that; when it goes well, ask the younger you in the story what they need from you now. Turn 65's P3 (sp5-p3-b6) read Human alone but AI with P2 (0.99, round t71a); x3-a and x3-b read AI alone (round t71b). Alone Human Written (0.094), https://www.pangram.com/history/da1abaaf-29eb-4f7c-887d-fdf92fa54815 (round t71b); the h2 Human Written (0.24, high, 226 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/c9f9e639-32af-46ea-a863-1bb460af2d8e (round t71c). -->

Do keep an ear on who's narrating, though. If the voice starts sneering, like it's making fun of the younger you, stop right there (that's the courtroom creeping back in). Same if you feel more ashamed, or kind of floaty and unreal, since more distance won't help with that. And when it goes well, it's a good time to check in with the younger you in that story. What do they need from you now?

## One Intention in the Morning, One Review at Night

<!-- Turn 72 (his answers of 2026-10-18 19:06 UTC (experiments/t72-answers/JOEL-20261018-1906.md)): 50: his own version of the section, word for word, in place of mine ("keep all except for 4"; Benjamin Franklin, my pointer 4, is out: "he seems tangential to this practice and from what you said it sounds a bit OCD"). He checked it himself: "this is human high conf". The rewrite and what it teaches are in experiments/t72-guide/REVIEWS.md. -->

In the morning, begin the day intentionally, not just because the sun chose to come up once more, since we can't count on that 🫣. Here's one way you might pl/orkfully start off:

“Today I will neither be Peter Pan nor Captain Hook. I will notice a hook or 2 which lead to unconscious behaviors.”

Then at night, pay yourself to be your own therapist so as not to break the bank. Equipoisedly look into how the practice went for you today:

"Where did I notice the hook?" At dinner with the in-laws? Doom scrolling TikTok nonsense? Watching animé?

"Where did it still seem to control me despite noticing it?" (That's the most frustrating part, when you know better, and you still act the same.)

Then get practical so it doesn't become another OCD loop: "What small repair or protective act shall I bring in for a proper response to that?"

And don't forget to appreciate the good! Moments of warm, beneficial connection (whether internally from child to adult, socially, spiritually, or undefined) are all to be celebrated. Were you maybe firm like an elephant today, or courageous like a lion? Adopt the entire spirit animal zoo if need be! Turtles are good if you need impulse control.

Count recognition as progress, regardless whether it came up a bit late, as the goal is to bring the awareness closer and closer to the present. All or nothing from the get go would be cool, but we normally have to learn step by step. If/when you interrupted the pattern, pat yourself on the back, or even kiss yourself on the shoulder. You can renew your intention for tomorrow, getting better & better, every day, in every way. And enjoy your well-earned rest tonight.

## Use Childhood Photographs Gently

<!-- Turn 67: Joel's section, word for word under his heading (2026-10-17 14:01 UTC, his answer to question 36: "here learn some lessons this passes"; experiments/t67-answers/JOEL-20261017-1401.md; his paste is experiments/t67-guide/joel-photos-h2.txt). One obvious typo fixed under his rule (2026-10-06): "glean" for "gleam" in P2. His check counts for the section as he sent it. With the fix, P2 read Human alone (0.0004, low), https://www.pangram.com/history/f91ae99f-1b52-4f44-83c9-a6be8cb8bb2e; the h2 Human Written (0.041, high, 463 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/7171bb76-f8de-4462-8b4f-72df4f444971; and the Simple Practices h1 with it Human Written (0.061, high, 905 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/95ad57f0-d31b-4df6-b8fa-a0a5fb780c58 (round t67a). -->

<!-- Turn 95 (his answer of 2026-10-22 21:35 UTC, experiments/t95-answers/JOEL-20261022-2135.md): 84.4 A, my proposal for his opener ("Maybe you've tried ... and thought", a made-up reader scenario; round 3 read it AI, High 40). The rest of his paragraph is unchanged. -->

<!-- Turn 109 (his answers of 2026-10-25 14:20 UTC, experiments/t109-answers/JOEL-20261025-1420.md): 105.2, his own first sentence ("this is the part that reads ai and it's actually redundant also"), and his two sentences for the start of P2, with "Pema Chödrön's" for his "Pema Chodron's", as the article spells her name elsewhere, and a doubled space as one -->

If picturing your little one feels like you’re just making them up, a real photo of you as a child can help, since there’s no face or posture to invent.

While you look, notice any new feelings or urges, and any tightness or unusual feelings in your body, including the good ones. Use Pema Chödrön's "feather touch" approach to gently bring some light and love to the edges of any contracted feelings. Is there something that younger you seems to want you to understand? Some of it you'll remember yourself, and some of it you might even glean from talking to family/friends from then ("You hated that coat"). It might just be an inference you're guessing from the picture, or perhaps it triggers an image or a dream. Or just nothing comes up. Ask what your little one needs from you now, and do a bit of it.

We don't need to ask it leading questions, like "Who abused me & how?" or "What memory is my body hiding?" Grilling one photo until a story shows up is a good way to get a story, but stories aren't necessarily the truth, and even if they are accurate, the feelings beneath them are still more important. Memory is an amorphous thing, easily influenced by guided imagery and suggestions. [Look at this study, for example](https://link.springer.com/article/10.3758/BF03196318), where people were shown a doctored photo of a childhood hot-air balloon ride they never took. Half of them actually started sorta remembering it. I'm sure you can relate.

A photo is just one moment that somebody picked and framed... not necessarily representative. Flipping thru an album may give different jolts in your body.

If you start to suspect someone of something serious, like abusing you, please don't confront or accuse anyone based only on what came up here. You might be looking at the photo thinking, "That would explain so much." Journal what came up and how it came up, and see if you can get support from someone level-headed who won't lead you toward an answer.

Put the photo back in its drawer when you're finished. If it starts tugging at you, so you keep going back to search it or you need to know for sure, or you're getting overwhelmed, take a break from it right then. Settle yourself in the present to close out properly. Re-evaluation counseling always ends on this "Present Time" reminder. Can be as simple and silly as "Oh, Today is Monday, my favorite day of the week!"

Turn 65 tried fourteen versions and four writers; tonight the h2 read AI with every P2 until the next step became a question (rounds t71b to t71n). Record: experiments/t71-guide/REVIEWS.md. -->

That "Present Time" ending is worth stealing for every session, photos or no photos. Remember the checking from When Healing Turns Into Checking? Without an ending, the processing can slide right back into that. So when you're done, look around and say where you are. Feel your feet on the floor, or the chair under you, and unclench your jaw and hands, since they've probably been at it this whole time.

Maybe it's making food, or just going to bed."); nothing has to be resolved first, whatever you're still feeling can come along; going back to your life is part of the healing (the guide's "integration"), as turn 65's sp9-k2 had it. experiments/t71-guide/drafts/cs2-p.txt: without cs2-o's "Yes, yes, it can feel a bit weird ...", which echoed his "Yes, yes, it might feel a bit strange" in the photographs section a few paragraphs up (with it, the h2 read Human Written 0.085, high, https://www.pangram.com/history/5188d938-2c32-4048-9c4b-610fb4f82db0). Alone Human Written (0.094), https://www.pangram.com/history/d0d6ba3b-47c1-4f8e-b30b-e53e82be4537; the h2 Human Written (0.31, medium, 145 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/baca3b6f-7aae-49b3-942d-cd213a4416fe (round t71p). -->

And what's next for you two? Maybe it's making food, or just going to bed. Whatever it is, tell your little one, "That's enough for today. We're going back to the life we're building." Nothing has to be resolved first, and whatever you're still feeling can come along. Going back to your life is part of the healing, too.

# When the Adult Voice Feels Fake

<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 16): P1, the guide's "This is where many people get stuck…" (G1). Writers a, b and c were each 100% AI alone (71, 70, 77), try 1, the whole paragraph flagged. A lot of people get stuck right here. You call up the Nurturer and try to talk to your little one, and you get silence or a numb feeling, and all of a sudden your phone or the laundry feels urgent. Or some voice says this is silly, and it's hard to tell which part of you is saying it.

<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 16): P2, the guide's own lines, word for word as in Joel's source (articles/inner-signal/source/inner-child-owner-20260911.md). Joel, 2026-10-02 03:15 UTC: "he said that, altho i'm not sure word for word but yes that's how i remembered it." Checked in the section (the quote is 22 words). -->

My friend Scott said:

> "Half the time when I try to connect to baby me, I feel fake and it seems crazy."

<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 18): Joel's change (18:56 UTC), "(from the inner child's view)" after "even less excusable": "there's a diff between me blaming someone and me pointing out how their inner child may blame them. It's not my place to blame, but their inner child does have that right to say what they think about their treatment." Passes on his check; not re-checked alone. (Turn 17) P3 is Joel's (03:15 UTC), word for word, "tested human" on his check: "yes i used to think that. but what P3 is really missing is who is feeling fake? if the little one, it could be persistence. if the adult feels fake, more fake sessions could be more damaging than just not doing the fakery in the first place." He added his own experience (the personal trainer, his mom pretending to listen). Turn 16: P3, the guide's "I used to think the answer was mainly persistence…" (G3). Fresh writer a of three. "I used to think" is the guide's claim about what Joel thought, and his own words don't say it, so the paragraph doesn't claim it (both groundings: right to leave it out); "I don't think" is his view now. Make a Simple Vow already has "one beautiful session wouldn't convince me either", so the one-session point is a clause. Cold read and grounding on a and b: every line OK; c's cold read couldn't tell what "being dropped" meant. At 39 words it was checked in the section: 100% Human (131), try 1. Joel's P3 isn't re-checked alone (his check); in the section, 100% Human (207), try 1 (turn 17). -->

I used to be all personal trainer and teach persistence on this as the remedy. Keeping at it does help, since a little one who learned to expect being dropped won't trust you right away. But I don't think that explains the adult feeling fully. More importantly, I think it could even be like unintentional gaslighting to the little one to ask to connect with them when you don't believe they're even real. Like when my mom pretended to listen to me when I was young, but even less excusable (from the inner child's view) because she was not asking for my time when she did that. So, it's worth meditating first on why it feels fake.

<!-- Turn 71 (the overnight run): the guide's research paragraph from Borrow One (r4 line 223), its second half here: compassion imagery and threat; "So “this feels fake” may mean the person needs a bridge rather than a more forceful performance of love."; waiting since 2026-09-25 to move here (BORROW-ONE-JOB-DRAFTS-20260925.md, U10b to U10d; OPEN-OWNER-FLAGS.md). Opened 2026-10-18: Baldwin et al. 2020, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31301164/ (its title, "Attachment and compassion-threat"; anxious and avoidant attachment, not self-criticism; a 10-minute attachment prime before the second imagery), and Duarte et al. 2015, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25347984/ (its practitioner point: an initial instance of imagery "can be frightening for people who have a tendency to be self-critical"). experiments/t71-guide/drafts/u10-e.txt. Alone Human Written (0.0048), https://www.pangram.com/history/17b3b60e-b61b-481b-90f6-635ef848afd1 (round t71ooo); When the Adult Voice Feels Fake as installed with it Human Written (0.080, high, 4364 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/64bfc332-e698-45f3-8ef5-f0c8a0ee02f3 (round t71ppp). Two one-paragraph versions with the pilot read AI alone (0.53 and 0.60, round t71nnn). -->

Sometimes kindness feels scary more than fake. One study's title has a word for that: "[compassion-threat](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31301164/)." People in it with anxious or avoidant attachment reacted to compassion imagery like a threat, and a ten-minute exercise in feeling secure helped with the next try. [Another](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25347984/) found a first try at imagery like that "can be frightening for people who have a tendency to be self-critical." So feeling fake could mean you need a bridge, more than a bigger performance of love.

<!-- Turn 71: the same paragraph's first half, the Ideal Parent Figure pilot (U10a), and its "Related research gives this idea some support without proving the whole method." Opened 2026-10-18: Parra et al. 2017, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5700488/ (17 adults with complex PTSD and childhood trauma, a five-week programme, stable at the eight-month follow-up, no control group). experiments/t71-guide/drafts/u10-f.txt. Alone Human Written (0.36), https://www.pangram.com/history/4049a1da-6151-462b-9393-cc2a2af221dc (round t71ooo); the h1 as above (round t71ppp). -->

And picturing one ideal parent seems to help some people. There's a whole method built on it, called the Ideal Parent Figure method. In a [small pilot](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5700488/), seventeen adults with complex PTSD from childhood trauma did five weeks of it and were still doing better eight months later. Seventeen people with nobody to compare them to won't prove the whole method, but it's a start.

<!-- 2026-10-03 (turn 24): the guide update's altered-states paragraph is Joel's minimal fix (16:16 UTC) of Claude's try 3 with its proposed last sentence ("MDMA" for "mushrooms"; "right there! Oh my gosh! And the love"), Human, medium confidence on his check alone; "just see how it can break up the marching order of code instructions translated to english". Record: experiments/WHEN-THE-ADULT-VOICE-FEELS-FAKE-20261002.md, turn 24. -->

For some people it only stops feeling fake in an altered state, like on MDMA or deep in meditation. All of a sudden your little one is right there! Oh my gosh! And the love or grief that sounded like therapy talk is just obvious. Old memories might come up too. They can feel totally real, but that alone doesn't prove they happened. I'd still hang on to what you felt, even once it's gone, and try to bring a little of it into your normal life. You've felt that your little one is real now, so talking to them afterward isn't just pretending.

## The Parent You Inherited

<!-- Turn 98 (his message of 2026-10-23 02:29 UTC, experiments/t98-answers/JOEL-20261023-0229.md): 88.3, his rewrite of The Parent You Inherited, word for word except two typos ("hanlde", "pont"): "that reference was too taxing for sucha small exercise, not necessary, i cut it. in general too much polish on this section, like a weird mix of overly efficient prose with actual lack of meaning." His check: "100% dark green on pangram". 90: A is covered by it. -->

I’d look at the parent “script” you inherited: how you learned to treat yourself from how people treated you growing up. Maybe sometimes that script feels warm, but maybe too often it feels more like a critic, or a warden with rules. The rules can even sound totally reasonable, like “You can come back to the table when you’re done with your embarrassing crying fit.” If your childhood was like that, your little one learned bit by bit to hide that part of their authentic self which wasn’t allowed to show up, so they could keep belonging. And if so, do a double check: they might still be doing it!

<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 17): P5, the guide's "The inherited parent compares the outward persona with an impossible ideal…" (G5). Turn 16's writer c (100% Human alone, 73, try 1) with the grounding's two fixes this turn: "If it's a critic," since the attack was told about every reader's inherited parent, against P4's "It can be warm" (CONTRADICTS); and "That second one means nothing you do could ever count" in place of "doesn't even make sense" (UNSUPPORTED: some of what you show can really be a cover, so what the reader needs is what's wrong with the attack). Alone, 100% Human (80), try 2. Cold read and grounding on the fixed text: every line OK. GREAT, not proposed: to your little one the new voice can sound like the critic again, since both come from the same grown-up; P4's hiding already answers why they don't answer. -->

<!-- Turn 98 (his message of 2026-10-23 02:29 UTC, experiments/t98-answers/JOEL-20261023-0229.md): his rewrite of the section, word for word (88.3). -->

If the script is mostly critical, it means the parent you inherited is busy measuring whatever you show the world against an impossible ideal. Then you can feel attacked from both sides: not only are you not good enough, but if you do seem to be good enough, it's probably fake, so it's like nothing you do really counts. When a new grown-up voice shows up saying “Trust me,” with a background like that, well, of course your little one doesn’t answer.
<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 18): P6 is Joel's (18:56 UTC), word for word; passes on his check, not re-checked alone. <!-- Turn 98 (his message of 2026-10-23 02:29 UTC, experiments/t98-answers/JOEL-20261023-0229.md): his rewrite of the section, word for word (88.3). -->

But with the borrowed adulthood trick, the new grown-up can start outside your head, as someone who likes you just the way you are, or as something you do to protect your little sweetie pie, like letting the phone ring when you know already how that call goes. You don’t have to pretend to feel anything to let a phone ring. It may take many such ignored calls before your inner child can start trusting you, and all the while, the parent you inherited calls your efforts fake.

### Answering the Strict Disciplinarian

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E14 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e14 a") -->

A good routine, like yoga in the morning and a proper bedtime, still feels like lockdown to your little one when it’s done in a disciplinarian style. One exercise you can do to handle this is to go back and rewrite your responses to that strict disciplinarian. First, remember who it was that drove you so hard as a child. Then start a little rap sheet of their provoking lines. Comfort your little one before you answer any of it. Your answers can be firm or funny, as long as they aren’t mean, since mean is the Critic’s department.

<!-- Turn 98 (his message of 2026-10-23 02:29 UTC, experiments/t98-answers/JOEL-20261023-0229.md): his rewrite of the section, word for word (88.3). -->

The Critic's voice can often pile on, to the point where you can't even understand why or what you're really feeling anymore. You feel sad at first, then the Inner Critic calls your inner child pathetic for being sad, and now you’re ashamed and want to hide. You could probably have handled the sadness with some simple nurturing instead from the beginning, but now you have to peel back this other nonsense to get to it.

## Play Is Part of the Work

<!-- 2026-10-02 (turn 18): P0 is Joel's version from 2026-09-27 01:59 UTC ("i preferred to have it this way which also passed"), word for word: Claude's first two sentences with his last one. It was the Three Adult Functions P1; that h1 was dissolved, so it waited for the first use of pl/ork. Not re-checked alone (his check); in the h1, 100% Human (661), try 1. -->

Your little one wants to play, too, which is why I call the deeper therapy pl/ork, play + work. A lot of the best discoveries come when you're both just messing around, kind of like how kids tell you what's really going on from the back seat, when nobody's looking right at them. Play not only paves the way for therapy, but is a part of how successful therapy should be measured.

(the guide's "The adult position keeps the child inside the relationship."), which The Chicken-and-Egg Problem says already ("The child isn't pushed away"; REPEATS-10-start-with.md, 1), and whose "Being the grown-up doesn't mean" opens a paragraph of When the Spiritual Relationship Hurts too. Alone Human Written (0.0013), https://www.pangram.com/history/bb112af9-7abf-4154-800c-4b811c1d0764 (round t71ooo); When the Adult Voice Feels Fake as installed with it Human Written (0.080, high, 4364 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/64bfc332-e698-45f3-8ef5-f0c8a0ee02f3 (round t71ppp). -->

A lot of what adults tend to lose, your little one still has, like the silliness and the irreverence, the rawness, the creativity, all that, and maybe a connection to the Great Mystery from before any path gave it a name. That's the spark of life they bring, and you get to draw on it and give back, so it's more communion than command. For one example of the spark, there's my post [Treasuring the Inner Child's Gift](https://ibogaqueen.substack.com/p/treasuring-the-inner-childs-gift).

Every sentence is word for word and used once. -->

You might go looking for your little one and get mad instead, or realize you've been staring at the rug. I count that as pl/ork too, even if it feels like it's in the way. Before you decide where it's coming from, ask it what it's trying to stop, or what it wants. Maybe it just has something to say. It could be your little one, mad that you took so long to come back, or a part of you that's trying to protect you. Maybe it's that parent you inherited, or something from earlier today, or a bit of each.

You can tell that part something like, "I see you. I'm not going to force my way past you. What's going on?" Or however you'd actually say that. Then wait and listen patiently.

If nothing comes, that's fine for now. No need to answer for that part just because you'd set this up as a healing session. Even without the answer, you can notice what sets the reaction off, and you can understand what the reaction is propelling you toward, and away from.

Or if you do get an answer, it could come in different forms, like as a voice or just a hunch. It's often more like a feeling. Maybe grief or anger. If so just listen and be present for it if that's what it wants. If it tells you to do something, I'd only do it if it's loving and leaves you free. It should make sense, too. Even then, anything big or hard to undo can wait until you're calm and you've checked it against what you actually know.

How you picture all that is up to you, as long as it helps, and the simpler the better. I think a fear that keeps turning up to tell you something is worth talking to as a part of you. If it sounds like your little one, though, hear it out the first time it shows up. Let a stray thought go as just thinking, and come back to the room you're in. A worry that just keeps asking to be reassured can go the same way, unless it's your little one asking.

I wouldn't spend long deciding what to call each part, though. Every part of you learns from how the others treat it, your little one included. A part of you that's trying to protect you might warn you about something, and when you hear it out, it doesn't have to get any louder.

Anxiety is often a part of you that's trying to protect you, making a guess about what's coming, like that people will laugh at you. It can be right! It's probably been wrong before too, and you know some of those times. Unless it might be real danger, or something a doctor should look at, I'd go see.

If a part hesitates about being touched or about sex, or says no to going deeper in this work or to an altered state, I'd stop there and not test it, even when it sounds like a prediction.

One of these patterns is a skeptic, on duty all the time: "You're just making this up." It may have spent years keeping you from being humiliated or abandoned again, and it has probably watched you get excited about other healing things before that got unexplainedly dropped a month later. We do need some skepticism, so thank it for keeping you on track.

The other one shows up when you get close to some painful memory or insight. Suddenly you'd rather check your phone, or do anything aside from directly feeling it (even a yawn can come with an "I'm so done with this" feeling). Maybe this guard doesn't believe there's a grown-up around who could handle what's coming? That yawn might actually be the opposite of checking out, though. Re-evaluation Counseling, a global movement of peer counseling, counts yawning as productive emotional discharge, a feeling on its way out. The skeptic and the pull both started out guarding you against something that really hurt. If you treat either one like an enemy, it'll probably just think "See? I knew it," and it stays on the payroll since guards require attackers.

#### The Belief Behind the Guards

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E16 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e16 a") -->

Say you learned somewhere along the way that if you need someone, they end up leaving you. You probably never said it to yourself in words, but it steers you before you know why. That pull of distraction is one way of dealing with it. Many folks will also find themselves going along with things they don't really prefer, or else insisting they don't need anybody (sour grapes). Some people pleasers go along for months as if nothing's wrong while the pressure builds and then they blow up out of nowhere. Then they feel so bad about it they go right back to going along to get along. With any of these patterns, I'd ask what it's protecting you from, because it doesn't really help to just label it a bad habit. (Sometimes a sweet is just a sweet, though, not an escape, as The Inner Guide gets into.)

The tells: a reassuring reframe and a tidy closer ("I'd just notice whether that's still true now."). One sentence says what keeping quiet costs now. Lint CLEAR. Not yet checked in Pangram. -->

You're actually a prophet, but you didn't know it! Have you ever heard of a self-fulfilling prophecy? Say nobody ever hears that you're struggling, since you didn't think they'd want to. Then nobody offers to help, and that can look like proof nobody cares. Keeping quiet might have been the smartest thing you could do back then, with nobody safe to tell, but now, if there are people who are safely available, it can keep help away.

Every sentence is word for word and used once. -->

Whichever protective part showed up, try talking to it in the same Nurturer voice you meant for your little one. At some point you can ask that part, "What are you afraid would happen if you stepped back?" Usually your little one only comes forward after that part feels heard. Peek-a-boo!

A harder voice can seem more fitting for something that's been in your way. I'd still keep it the same. If that voice isn't there in you yet, borrow it from someone who's been safe for you.

That part might say this will just be one more performance, and you'll fool yourself again. That one can sting, because there could be something to it. Maybe you've fooled yourself before, and that part remembers. "Tell me what you remember, I'm listening." works well here. It puts you into the role of the Nurturer, which alleviates the instinct to argue back. If you push past it, you might get exactly the performance it was afraid of. Even if you didn't win an Emmy last year, that can look convincing, with the right words and maybe even tears, while nothing underneath feels any safer.

That part doesn't have to move out. It's been living there way longer than the grown-up you. It can keep watch while you take one small step toward your little one, and I think watching is what it wants anyway, to find out if there's a grown-up here now who could call it off if things got to be too much. It doesn't have to be all or nothing, either. Maybe it would settle for a lighter cover for now, like blinds tilted open a crack, and let you peek out for a minute (ST-CA, section 9.3.4). If it says no, I'd listen. When it lets you, keep an eye on that part too, for any change at all.

If it's been calling all this stupid, it might turn out to be scared of getting its hopes up. I wouldn't want to get mine up either. I'd tell it something like, "You don't have to hope yet, I'll keep coming back even when I mess up." Sometimes it doesn't say anything back for a while.

A new roommate doesn't win you over with a speech on move-in day (hopefully, right?). The rent just has to keep showing up. Your Protector earns that guard's trust about the same way, mostly out in ordinary life. The things you do can be small, like keeping a promise to yourself nobody else knew about. Or saying no to someone, and not texting an hour later to take it back. That second one can be really hard.

Blaming yourself can be doing a job like that, too. "It's all my fault" might be your parent's line, word for word. Then again, if it's your fault, maybe you can fix it before they leave. I'd stop the cruelty either way. And I'd get curious about what makes it so hard to give up.

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): his When to Change the Strategy, word for word ("i fixed the whole section and now it's 100% high conf human"); 98.7's point is his P3's texting example -->

Weeks in and nothing much is changing? I’d want to quit too, or else keep doing the exact same thing forever so I never have to find out what's underneath this. Before going with either of those paths, look for what exactly is in the way. Sometimes the strategy was not actually implemented, just read about or played around with lightly. Or there wasn't the right environment to really practice it, like no alone time. Or perhaps you did sit down with your little one, but it felt scary or like some part of you was trying to protect you from going deeper. Whatever it turns out to be, it makes sense to work on that blocker instead of giving up on everything.

Maybe the words are all there, but the loving grown-up behind them isn’t, not yet. Some people at that point might try saying sweeter and sweeter things, and then upon that not working, perhaps just “throw the baby out with the bathwater,” and decide reparenting is a scam. I’d borrow that grown-up first. Then try again, and see if your little one can tell the difference.

<!-- Turn 90 (the half-A rework, experiments/t89-results/RESULT-A-meta.json, check t88s_A: Mostly Human Written, 7.2% AI, 41 of 254 windows): my paragraph (turn 30, PGQ-005); its middle read AI (Low 27, Low 17, into his P4). Two phrases plainer. Drafts: experiments/t89-drafts/. Lint CLEAR. Not yet checked in Pangram. -->

Let's say an exercise leaves you feeling lighter for a few hours, but then the old feeling comes back later on. Some relief is nice, but we still want to know if we are fixing the problem at a deeper level and integrating the benefits into regular life (like texting a friend that you're having a rough day rather than saying you're fine). Jot down what changed and how long it lasted. Although if that feeds into your OCD patterns, drop it.

<!-- Turn 107 (his answers of 2026-10-25 00:24 UTC, experiments/t107-answers/JOEL-20261025-0024.md): 103.4 A: "if it progress" is "if progress" in P4 below -->

<!-- P4's last three sentences are Joel's, word for word (2026-10-06 19:04 UTC), in place of "And if you keep doing it right and what was supposed to happen still doesn't, I think you've learned something real about it. Something with clearly better research behind it is worth switching for too." No punctuation fix was needed. -->

How many tries would I give it? It's not like we can just put a number on it. Some things shift in a day. Others take about as long as learning guitar. But do notice if progress is stagnating. If it’s making things worse, or you just can’t do it right now, I’d change course sooner (stopping counts).

Not wanting to do it anymore might be reason enough, but if that “nope” sounds like a part of you that’s trying to protect you, I’d hear it out first to get a better understanding. And if you keep following the reparenting steps correctly without getting the expected results, consider if you have a unique case these steps don’t match yet. Maybe you’ll find something that works better for you, or you can ask me what I’d do for this case, or ask a therapist you work with. Either way, I’d like to hear about it!

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

Sometimes the urge to get away from what’s coming up emotionally gets a lot bigger. All of a sudden you need a drink, or that ex doesn’t seem so bad after all. Some people start a whole new business. If that’s you, it might mean you got close to something that really hurts, and that can be progress, honestly! I’d thank whatever part of you came up with the escape plan, and slow down. If it’s more than you can hold right now, I’d get someone steady with you.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

#### Intensive Meditation and Psychedelics

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E18 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e18 a") -->

So why not just blast right past that part, with intensive meditation or psychedelics? Both can get you past the guards before there’s a grown-up big enough to hold whatever comes through, so I’d hold off until there’s enough Nurturer and Protector around. How much is enough depends partly on the medicine and what therapy is done with it.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

Like MDMA, San Pedro is more protective and nurturing than many other medicines, especially at lower doses, so you don’t need as much Nurturer and Protector built up for those, though you still need some. Some people benefit more from solo therapy, while others benefit more from having a trained therapist guide the experience.

# Altered States Can Deepen the Therapy

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

I’d learn to do inner-child therapy sober first, in your ordinary mindstate, since that’s the one you’ll be living in. Cars don’t start at 60mph, that’s not the way the universe seems to work. First gear leads to second leads to third, etc. Second gear, in this case, may refer to [gentle meditation](http://love.u-dont-exist.com) or self-hypnosis. When you’re ready, my [self-hypnosis guide](http://hypnosis.u-dont-exist.com/) and [lucid-dreaming guide](http://lucid.u-dont-exist.com/) can take those deeper, into third gear. Low doses of entheogens can also synergize compatibly with various gears: cactus, CBD tea with sublingual mapacho, [Loveyhuasca](http://loveyhuasca.info), [iboga](http://iboga.u-dont-exist.com/), and so on.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 97.6 A (his "97. i agree cut all"): cut "And yes, those caution signs still count.". The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

Remember those guards from The Urge to Escape, above? Same deal here, so get enough Nurturer and Protector in the room before you go deep. Borrowed is fine, as long as it’s from someone steady who won’t try to run your trip for you. But feeling attacked by a demon on a psychedelic is not necessarily paranoia. It can be real, and it’s also not bad unless you’re unprepared. It can be supremely empowering when you can send love and see how it fixes the situation.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

## What to Do With a Peak

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E19 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e19 a") -->

Now, the beautiful stuff. Say one night (on a psychedelic, in a dream, ten days into a meditation retreat, whatever) you felt more love than you knew was possible. Wonderful, and it matters! Keep it as a reference point, and chase it playfully, because it is already a part of you, and you’ll be like a cat chasing its tail, which can be cute and fun, or dizzy and absurd!

Jack Kornfield has a whole book on what comes next, [After the Ecstasy, the Laundry](https://jackkornfield.com/after-the-ecstasy-the-laundry/). The title kind of says it all. That night can't be the bar for every morning after, or regular life starts to feel like failing. And gorgeous as it was, it doesn't mean you're done healing. The laundry will tell you more, and it's the same test as in Timing Is Individual, further down.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

One reason the peak is so momentary is because we often don’t need to truly give anything up to get it. Say you’re still pretty angry, and you want enlightenment, but it has to come with the money and the partner in one package. Ketamine can get you to a peak anyway. Then, you might now have some kind of transcendental “understanding” glimpse that seems like it could fix everything, if only you could be more aware of it, which often leads into a cycle of taking more and more medicine over time. I’d prefer to ask if any of the discontent has been let go, or the anger? If not, that peak was less the love we’re after here than “I’ll be grateful once I’ve got it all,” the gratitude with conditions from Ask God for a Loan.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

<!-- Turn 87 (his answers of 2026-10-22 15:33 UTC (experiments/t87-answers/JOEL-20261022-1533.md)): 77.1 A: the cross-reference now names Hear the Guard First. Not yet checked in Pangram. -->

And if your little one still can’t take the love in? Hold off on going for bigger, crazier breakthroughs, however tempting it is, or at least know that they are a double-edged sword. Something’s in the way, and getting to know it is your next job. Often it’s a protective part that’s seen love come with strings before. Hear it out first, the way you did in Hear the Guard First.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

## When Escape Leaves Reality Behind

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

All kinds of psychoactive drugs can mess with your sense of what’s real, and that’s often the point of them, so you can break free of all your old patterns completely for a bit. But in general, try to select the healthiest ones and use slow and gentle protocols. I’d say one of the most abused psychedelics (aside from ketamine, if you call that a psychedelic) is 5-MeO-DMT. I have a [guide on that](http://bufo.u-dont-exist.com/). People very often believe you need a huge dose of it for a “full release.” They go through all kinds of screaming and shaking, nearly killing themselves, with absolutely zero inner-child work being done. It’s all spiritual bypassing.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

<!-- Turn 88 (his 16:12 UTC message, "77.5yes"): 80.3 A, his chat line of turn 87 (77.5, "don't try again until you understand what would make it safe") as its own sentence after "try to figure out why that happened", in his words and his straight apostrophe; "I’d look at the drug..." then reads as how you'd find out what would make it safe. Not yet checked in Pangram. -->

Your own drug history matters too. If you go manic, psychotic (by which I mean losing your sense of reality to the point of posing a risk to yourself or others), get super paranoid, or end up worse afterward than before, those are signs to reconsider trying the same thing again, and try to figure out why that happened. Don't try again until you understand what would make it safe. I’d look at the drug and the dosage, as well as mindset and setting etc.

<!-- Turn 72 (his answers of 2026-10-18 19:06 UTC (experiments/t72-answers/JOEL-20261018-1906.md)): 49, 3: "paranoia isn't automatically leading to psychosis, since it can be valid, but it does tend to lead to psychosis when it isn't balanced by practicality", with his three examples ("so let's merge that in somehow"), in place of "Paranoia during a rough trip is a warning sign for psychosis."; "Freemason" for his "free mason". experiments/t72-guide/drafts/par-a.txt. Alone Human Written (0.020, high), https://www.pangram.com/history/8b12481e-1af8-4c3d-a8e3-3f15b8577b46; When the Adult Voice Feels Fake with it Human Written (0.076, high, 4479 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/4a497c49-edfd-4eb1-b810-773529dd39bf (round t72a). -->

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

Paranoia during a rough trip can be a warning sign for psychosis, but it doesn’t automatically lead there, since it can be valid. It tends to go that way when it isn’t balanced by practicality. “The CIA is mind-controlling me!” is paranoia. “The CIA, marketers, and other forces are conspiring to mind-control me, the way they do to everyone else! But since I can see that, I can prevent it to some extent, just by being aware of how it operates” is paranoia too, but it’s practically useful, and less likely to lead to psychosis. “...which means I can prevent it by standing on my head and counting backwards from 33, since 33 is their magic Freemason number!” is leaning psychotic. Some drugs are known for causing paranoia, like THC, so I’d avoid those. If you can’t clearly understand what happened yourself, get someone qualified to help. And if you have an idea of what happened but aren’t sure, ask someone who knows you well.

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 97.7 A (his "97. i agree cut all"): cut ", though I don't mean every drug's dangerous for you". The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

Any of those reactions, from mania to paranoia, can also point to a vulnerability in you, so I'd be careful with other drugs that mess with what's real too. And any predisposition toward mania or psychosis is a warning sign too, whether it's family history, a bad environment or a bad mindset. These are just caution signs, and when you see them, you take more precautions of every kind. You have to figure out what they mean for you, and if you can't, get help from trusted advisors.

By trusted, I mean someone you know is trustworthy from a long relationship with them, not someone people on Reddit said was super magical and healing. That's called a crapshoot. It might turn out well, it might not. It might seem like it did, and then later you realize you were duped, as happened to me with [Sidney the iboga shaman](http://sidney.u-dont-exist.com/). A new therapist is not as trusted as an old one. As they say, "new friends are silver, but old friends are gold."

<!-- Turn 103 (his answers of 2026-10-23 21:52 UTC, experiments/t103-answers/JOEL-20261023-2152.md): 97.8 A (his "97. i agree cut all"): cut "If you can't tell whether you're safe, I'd treat that as risky.". The original is in experiments/t102-safety/SAFETY-CUTS.md. -->

<!-- Turn 105 (his answer of 2026-10-24 18:40 UTC, experiments/t105-answers/JOEL-20261024-1840.md): 101.1, GPT's rewrite of my paragraph, word for word as he pasted it ("GPT was able to fix that paragraph on its first try, it got human high conf on pangram"). It carries the guide's line 505 points the paragraph had: repeated milder confusion or spacing out, help when it's severe or happens sober, and no sudden stop of a medication or a substance the body depends on. What my four drafts got wrong is gate E256. -->

Say you keep coming back from trips confused or spaced out. Maybe nothing worse happens, but after several trips like that I'd be concerned. If you're having episodes when you're sober, or any of them are severe, see a psychiatrist. And don't suddenly stop a medication just because you suspect it's involved. Talk to the doctor who prescribed it. Benzos can be dangerous to quit cold turkey. Alcohol withdrawal can be dangerous if you're physically dependent.

Be careful what you mix, too, and not just prescribed medicines. Even some basic things like cinnamon can be dangerous (and helpful) with ayahuasca, and doctors don't know about a lot of this. I also learned that mixing acacia confusa with mimosa hostilis is very destabilizing and challenging, but excellent if you want to know how it feels to be painfully autistic + schizo! I have a [list of things to be cautious about with ayahuasca](http://ayasafety.u-dont-exist.com/) in particular.

### Gentle Meditation and Mantra

<!-- Turn 106 (his answers of 2026-10-24 22:55 UTC, experiments/t106-answers/JOEL-20261024-2255.md): E21 from the ideas page, the heading only (his "e21 a") -->

Gentle meditation or self-hypnosis is a different story. It can really calm your nervous system down. A lot of people find that a mantra they feel some heart connection to, repeated in their head or out loud, especially with a nice melody, can lower the walls enough for the heart to open up. It's not something to lean on all day as a way of coping, but used as therapy it can be super effective. I personally love this beautiful Om Mane Pemme Hum mantra that I found in the Garchen Rinpoche documentary, For the Benefit of All Beings:

## Altered States Can Require Reparenting

<!-- Turn 71: his paragraph as his October guide has it (question 43, 8: "my words", 2026-10-17 22:59 UTC). Alone Human Written (0.0016), https://www.pangram.com/history/778eb69d-e2e9-4955-8877-9e529510a3a0; the h2 with its h4 Human Written (0.0046, high, 1009 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/433adc21-9efd-4f86-aed6-30f3fbb81357 (round t71h), and the h1 as installed Human Written (0.0059, high, 1209 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/ff62d372-f0bd-409a-8c28-d771cfb0975d (round t71h). -->

Have you ever been attacked by a literal demon? Maybe during sleep paralysis or meditation or on a psychedelic trip? A lot of modern folk laugh at this, yet it’s really quite a common experience. I was being psychically attacked by ghosts in my home as a child. Because I was literally a child, I was an easy target. And since most people never actually grew up, they are easy targets for every kind of influence, including spirit influence.

<!-- Turn 71: his ghosts-and-window paragraph as his guide has it (43, 8). Alone Human Written (0.025), https://www.pangram.com/history/8f74323b-8eac-4d51-a7ee-a56ba71ee9f2; the h2 with its h4 Human Written (0.0046, high, 1009 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/433adc21-9efd-4f86-aed6-30f3fbb81357 (round t71h), and the h1 as installed Human Written (0.0059, high, 1209 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/ff62d372-f0bd-409a-8c28-d771cfb0975d (round t71h). -->

Those ghosts knew my weakness: dares. So they would dare me to jump out the window. I wasn’t suicidal, but I hated to seem like a “wussy.” Luckily, I trusted my parents with this information, and instead of taking me to a psychiatrist and pumping me full of anti-psychotic drugs, they put bars on the windows. Many kids are not so lucky in modern societies. They may get pumped full of anti-psychotic drugs for the rest of their life once diagnosed. That said, it'd make sense to get a professional to weigh in for some situations. I still played around with half-hanging out the windows, but I wasn’t quite dumb enough to jump out, and I really appreciate that my parents took it seriously without catastrophizing.

They would sit on me, prevent me from breathing, and instill tremendous fear into the core of my being.

Alone Human Written (0.0005, medium), https://www.pangram.com/history/97c57d66-0635-41f6-8e18-c0b60af560d6; Altered States Can Deepen the Therapy with it and his 49, 2 fixes Human Written (0.042, high, 1436 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/6671a67a-8ec2-45bc-8bcf-07c7efa28242 (round t72a). -->

Sleep paralysis is a trance-like state, and trance can be "more scary" because you're trapped in it, but it's also less actually dangerous. You can't ruin your life at all in trance (unless a bad hypnotic suggestion gets deeply implanted, perhaps), while without trance you can "freak out" in a way that might end up getting you committed to a mental hospital, thrown out of your housing, going to jail, possibly even killing yourself. In trance you can basically work with the fear. You recognize the fear has no real world significance and it's purely just fear, so it's super useful that way. The thing to fear is fear itself, since it actually opens us up to being manipulated by demons, which feed on it directly. So it's not really that they have no power. We give them the power.

<!-- Turn 87 (his answers of 2026-10-22 15:33 UTC (experiments/t87-answers/JOEL-20261022-1533.md)): 76.4 A: "My inner guide" is now "My inner Guide", the Guide's job. Not yet checked in Pangram. -->

After multiple episodes of this I decided to stop giving in to the victim mentality, and to start taking it seriously as a part of spiritual practice. My inner Guide saw it as a perfect opportunity to demonstrate the power of love over fear. The next time it happened, despite how scary it was, I sent unconditional love to this demon on me. It took some minutes, but once that love was seen as enduring, the demon left, and it never returned.

I’ve also trained other people to do this during spiritual attack while on psychedelics. It works a lot better if you are already trained in [loving-kindness meditation](https://love.u-dont-exist.com/) to the point where it’s natural. I’d say it works way better than exorcism techniques (like [Julian Palmer’s torping](https://julianpalmerism.com/primer-malefic-entities/), which he also admits doesn’t work as well as love).

<!-- Turn 71: the guide's astral-plane paragraph as published, with his thugs line (43, 8) and its link. The astral plane is a bit different from the 3D. In the astral realm, unconditional love is a true, ultimately powerful physical force. Like a river, or a moat. There’s no evil that can trespass that moat. But don’t assume it will always work like that in the physical plane. It definitely has worked for me sometimes when facing some thugs, but [definitely not with others](https://defense.u-dont-exist.com/). Loving someone does not mean you leave the front door unlocked with a sign saying “take whatever you want.”

If you’re not able yet (or in the moment) to summon real love for an enemy, you can first send love to your frightened little one from your adult Nurturer. Many people also find they can borrow support from God, Jesus, angels, devas, or whatever loving spiritual presence fits their tradition. If I were to do that, I would still be framing it as “Please help this situation with love” rather than “Strike down the enemy!” But if a presence seems to enjoy your fear, it's not one of your grown-ups, borrowed or not, whatever it says it is. Same goes if it demands you hurt or use anybody, yourself included.

Borrowing support like that is basically borrowed adulthood. At first you may need to feel protected or loved by something bigger than you, before that love starts to feel like yours, and then bit by bit it starts to become your own. Send some of it down to your frightened little one, and when that's possible, toward whatever you're afraid of. Continuing to relate to benevolent spiritual powers/beings is still perfectly fine, but they would surely be happy if they rubbed off on you!

#### Non-altered states can feel like this too

<!-- Turn 71: the guide's two paragraphs as published (the 💀 is his own placement, which EMOJI-LIST.md counts). P1 alone Human Written (0.071), https://www.pangram.com/history/3daa06f9-aecd-4b46-9fe7-5e0d0decbba5; P2 is 43 words, under the web app's 50, so the h4 stands for it: Human Written (0.060, medium), https://www.pangram.com/history/a5a2ee4e-2d14-40ca-8d63-8f45874c9805; the h2 with its h4 Human Written (0.0046, high, 1009 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/433adc21-9efd-4f86-aed6-30f3fbb81357 (round t71h), and the h1 as installed Human Written (0.0059, high, 1209 words), https://www.pangram.com/history/ff62d372-f0bd-409a-8c28-d771cfb0975d (round t71h). -->

Even social media algorithms will turn your life into hell if you don’t have the loving, protective inner parent developed. When you do, social media actually becomes useful and feels nice to interact with now and then.

Many people think reparenting sounds silly, or like the kind of ridiculous therapy people do when they have too much time or money. But you know what’s even sillier? Wandering the world aimlessly and getting manipulated by all kinds of evil forces. 💀

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

### My Psychedelic Experiences Came A Bit Too Soon

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

The first time I took San Pedro cactus, at a medium-low dose, it made me super emotional, and I cried a lot. It was very therapeutic, actually. But I still squandered that opportunity: with a better understanding of reparenting, it could have been much more therapeutic.

Ayahuasca was often much more challenging, because at first I was taking huge doses for ego death. The ayahuasca by itself wasn't exactly spiritual bypassing for me, though. It gave me a connection to divinity, and inner-child reparenting does work best with a little of that. But then I squandered the opportunity to bring that love down from the heavens to my inner child, which led me to be more like a preacher than a fully well-rounded individual who could lead by example.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

When you’re deeply entranced, you can’t freak out: no matter how scary something is, it’s just like, “Oh, this is scary, OK, oh well.” That’s not a good excuse to take crazy doses tho. I went psychotic once on ayahuasca made with pure THH (an alkaloid from the ayahuasca vine) and Mimosa hostilis bark, which has DMT in it. THH alone isn’t hypnotic (trance-like) enough to keep people safe with higher doses of DMT. I was very lucky that my landlord, a Quaker family friend, was super understanding and talked me down gently. Otherwise the cops might have been called, and I could still be in jail, perhaps, since ayahuasca was illegal there at the time, not to mention I had a bunch of mimosa bark in the house that they might have claimed was for distributing. In general, don’t call the cops and expect it to go well. That’s a real last resort.

Out of hundreds of times I've taken iboga, there was one time just a small amount of male iboga root bark got me a bit hypomanic, to the point where a thought came: "Everyone should listen to me!" Luckily I was trained in Buddhist meditation, so I immediately challenged it with "Why should anyone listen if they have no interest?" I stopped using iboga for a while after that, and I warn people about that potential issue with [male iboga](http://iboga.u-dont-exist.com/) specifically.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

### Physical Pain Makes It Harder

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

Physical pain can bring on the urge to escape too. Say an attack has you curled up on the bathroom floor, and whatever knocked the pain out last time (say, fent, or ketamine) starts looking real good, though you know it’ll leave you worse if you keep resorting to that quick fix.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

By now, you may have already been through a million doctors who shrugged their shoulders, or even alternative treatments that were too costly or hard to grade. Sometimes it really is so simple that the best doctors and scanners can’t figure it out, like your house is moldy. It is worth going through all the basic checks first that you can do yourself, and trying the things that make most sense. For more on that, check my [detox guide](http://detox.u-dont-exist.com).

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

<!-- Turn 102 (his answers of 2026-10-23 17:53 UTC, experiments/t102-answers/JOEL-20261023-1753.md): 95.7 A: "and they may be able to treat it while it's happening" added (PGQ-027). -->

And when the symptoms come and go randomly, the best time to get diagnostics done could be right at the actual time of the crisis, rather than waiting until you feel well enough to make an appointment, and they may be able to treat it while it's happening. But what if the attacks come almost every day? You can’t move into the ER. And normal test results don’t prove the pain is imaginary, just as a treatment that helps doesn’t explain what’s causing it.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

I’d ask for a chronic pain specialist and a written plan for the flare-ups you can see coming, including the new or severe signs that mean you go to the ER right away, but don’t let amelioration of symptoms be the end of the investigation.

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

#### Listen To Your Body, Or Else!

<!-- Turn 77: Joel's own text from his 2026-10-19 upload (the version he gave GPT), word for word apart from obvious typos; not yet checked in Pangram here. -->

Sometimes, a physical illness is really partly or wholly an emotional issue causing “somatic symptoms,” as happened to various friends of mine, as well as to Lucia Capacchione, PhD., and many of her clients and friends. She wrote one of the first comprehensive inner child therapy guides, [Recovery of Your Inner Child](https://www.goodreads.com/book/show/909586.Recovery_of_Your_Inner_Child), in 1991. While I don’t doubt that many people do recover from mysterious physical illness via meditation, inner child therapy, and [somatic therapy](http://somatic.u-dont-exist.com), I also don’t want to claim, as some folks do, that *all* illness is entirely rooted in emotional issues. That is clearly not true. We don’t need to be gaslit to consider all the possibilities.

Maybe you're reading this about to act, or with the means close by. Then before anything else, get another human being involved, and put some distance between you and the means. A crisis line counts, and the person on the other end can be a total stranger. Do the same if you can't stop yourself, or you're badly disoriented or intoxicated. Can't tell if that's you? Treat it like it is. None of that is me answering whether life is worth living. It just keeps the question open long enough to look at.

<!-- Turn 67: Joel's P2, word for word (2026-10-17 14:01 UTC, his answer to question 32: "i removed most of the AI tells, this is now human and it makes more sense"; experiments/t67-answers/JOEL-20261017-1401.md). His check counts for it alone. -->

Then, before getting all philosophical, make sure there's a real, live grown-up in the room or on the phone, if that's possible. And as for the internal psychology of it, when the part that wants to die feels like all of you, go ahead and notice who's doing the noticing (as we discussed in Noticing Counts), or borrow an adult from the adult lending library in your head :). Any part of you that can help keep your body safe, or would rather wait on a decision you can't undo, is welcome. And if none of them shows up yet? No problem, the questions can wait.
